**Oscar Jack Piastri** ([/piˈæstriː/](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Help\:IPA/English) [*pee-AST-ree*](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Help\:Pronunciation_respelling_key), Italian: [\[ˈpjastri\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Help\:IPA/Italian); born 6 April 2001) is an Australian [racing driver](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Racing_driver) who competes in [Formula One](10854.md) for [McLaren](20994.md). Piastri has won nine [Formula One Grands Prix](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Formula_One_Grands_Prix) across four seasons.&#x20;

Born and raised in [Melbourne](17306237.md), Piastri began his career in [radio-controlled racing](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Radio-controlled_car) before moving into [karting](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Karting) aged 10, where he won several regional titles. Graduating to [junior formulae](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Junior_formulae) in 2016, Piastri won his first championship at the [2019 Formula Renault Eurocup](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2019_Formula_Renault_Eurocup) with [R-ace GP](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/R-ace_GP). He then won both the [2020 FIA Formula 3](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2020_FIA_Formula_3_Championship) and [2021 FIA Formula 2](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2021_Formula_2_Championship) Championships back-to-back with [Prema](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Prema_Racing), becoming the sixth driver in history to win the [GP2](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/GP2_Series)/[Formula 2](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/FIA_Formula_2_Championship) title in their rookie season. Piastri is the only driver in history to win [Formula Renault](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Formula_Renault), [Formula Three](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Formula_Three), and [Formula Two](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Formula_Two)—or equivalent—championships in successive seasons.&#x20;

A member of the [Alpine Academy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Alpine_Academy) from 2020 to 2022,[^cite_note-RSA-2] Piastri signed with [McLaren](20994.md) in [2023](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2023_Formula_One_World_Championship) to partner [Lando Norris](48978813.md), following a contract dispute with [Alpine](65240620.md). He made his Formula One debut at the [Bahrain Grand Prix](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2023_Bahrain_Grand_Prix), achieving his first career podium in his rookie season at the [Japanese Grand Prix](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2023_Japanese_Grand_Prix). Retaining his [seat](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Glossary_of_motorsport_terms#S) for [2024](72504121.md), Piastri achieved his maiden [victory](6732460.md) in [Hungary](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2024_Hungarian_Grand_Prix), which he repeated in [Azerbaijan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2024_Azerbaijan_Grand_Prix), becoming the [fifth Australian to win a Formula One Grand Prix](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Formula_One_drivers_from_Australia#World_champions_and_race_winners). In [2025](72768531.md), he took seven further victories, as well as his maiden [pole position](24385119.md) at the [Chinese Grand Prix](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2025_Chinese_Grand_Prix), as he finished third in a title battle with Norris and [Max Verstappen](41758713.md).&#x20;

As of the [2026 Bahrain Grand Prix](80201648.md), Piastri has achieved nine race wins, six pole positions, nine fastest laps, and 28 podiums in Formula One. Piastri is contracted to remain at McLaren until at least the end of the 2028 season.&#x20;

## Early life

Piastri's Tatuus FR2.0/13 in the paddock of the 2018 Eurocup Formula Renault Monza round with its nose folded upwards

Piastri was sponsored by his father's company, *HP Tuners*, throughout his [junior formulae](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Junior_formulae) career.

Oscar Jack Piastri was born on 6 April 2001 in [Melbourne](17306237.md), Victoria, Australia.[^cite_note-RS2025-3] His father, Chris Piastri, is the founder and owner of *HP Tuners*, an [automotive software](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vehicle_diagnostics) company; the company sponsored his [junior formula](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Junior_formulae) career with up to [A\$](81215.md)6.5 million and continues to sponsor Oscar to this day.[^cite_note-SMH2021-4] He served as Piastri's [kart](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kart) mechanic as he contested [national championships in Australia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_kart_racing_championships#Australia).[^cite_note-NYT2023-5] His mother, Nicole Piastri ([née](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Birth_name#Maiden_and_married_names) MacFadyen), helped raise him in the suburb of [Brighton](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Brighton,_Victoria) with his three younger sisters—Hattie, Edie, and Mae.[^cite_note-6] He claims [Italian](385155.md), [Yugoslavian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Yugoslavs), and [Chinese](372983.md) heritage from his father, as well as [Scottish](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Scottish_people) and [Irish](775859.md) from his mother.[^cite_note-7] His bedtime stories frequently consisted of automotive books, prompting his father to buy him a [radio-controlled monster truck](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Radio-controlled_car) on a business trip when he was six, which he began racing in his backyard.[^cite_note-RS2025-3]&#x20;

Piastri raced competitively with the monster truck in 2007, before moving to race RC cars for 2008 through 2011 from ages seven to ten. He raced competitively aged nine with *Remote Control Racing Australia*, winning the secondary class of the national championship before moving into [kart racing](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kart_racing).[^cite_note-AS2024-8][^cite_note-FA2024-9] Aged 14, he moved with his father—who returned to Melbourne six months later—to [Hertford](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hertford), England, to continue his international racing career in Europe.[^cite_note-RS2025-3] Having been privately educated at [Haileybury](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Haileybury_\(Melbourne\)), he moved to [its sister school](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Haileybury_and_Imperial_Service_College) in [Hertford Heath](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hertford_Heath)—whose [alumni](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_people_educated_at_Haileybury_and_Imperial_Service_College) include [Stirling Moss](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Stirling_Moss)—as a [boarding pupil](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Boarding_pupil) on a [sports scholarship](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sports_scholarship).[^cite_note-10][^cite_note-11] By the end of 2015, Piastri began travelling for karting tests and competitions between Italy, France, Belgium, Sweden, Spain, and Portugal from [Stansted Airport](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Stansted_Airport), costing his father around [£](270673.md)250,000.[^cite_note-SMH2021-4] In [2019](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2019_Formula_Renault_Eurocup), he was taken under the wing of nine-time Grand Prix winner [Mark Webber](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mark_Webber_\(racing_driver\)) and his wife, Ann; the former has served as his [manager](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sports_agent) since and negotiated his move to Formula One.[^cite_note-AS2025-1-12]&#x20;

## Junior racing career

### Karting (2011–2016)

#### 2011–2015: State titles in Australia

Following his successes in [radio-controlled racing](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Radio-controlled_car), Piastri moved into [kart racing](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kart_racing) in 2011 at the local *Oakleigh Go Kart Racing Club* (OGKRC) in [Clayton South](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Clayton_South), aged 10.[^cite_note-SC2023-13][^cite_note-FA2024-9] A [protégé](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Prot%C3%A9g%C3%A9) of two-time Australian Champion James Sera,[^cite_note-AS2024-8] he began competing for the club that year in an [FA Kart](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/FA_Kart) chassis.[^cite_note-NYT2023-5][^cite_note-14] He won his first title two years later: the 2013 [CIK](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Commission_Internationale_de_Karting) Stars of Karting in the Rookies class.[^cite_note-NYT2023-5][^cite_note-15] In 2014, he won the OGKRC Championship, [City of Melbourne](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/City_of_Melbourne) Titles, [South Australian](26716.md) Championship, and National Sprint Classic Champion of Champions in Junior Clubman—he claimed a [A\$](81215.md)500 prize for the latter.[^cite_note-16][^cite_note-17][^cite_note-18] He further claimed runner-up in the Australian National Sprint Championship and the [Victorian](4689460.md) Championship, before progressing to [international competition](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_kart_racing_championships#Active_international_championships) in the IAME International Final at [Le Mans](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Circuit_de_la_Sarthe); he finished third on his debut in X30 Junior from twenty-first on the [grid](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Glossary_of_motorsport_terms#G).[^cite_note-19] He closed his national career with third in the [KF3](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/KF3) Australian Championship—where he was ranked the *Top Qualifier*—as well as victory in the Victorian Championship, OGKRC's Junior Top Guns, the [Gold Coast](59251.md) Race of Stars, and the City of Melbourne Titles again in 2015.[^cite_note-BRDC-20][^cite_note-KAU2015-21]&#x20;

#### 2015–2016: Move to the European scene

> I knew it was a necessary step if I wanted to have a chance to make it to Formula One; you can't become a Formula One driver by staying in Australia.&#x20;

— Piastri discussing his move to Europe with *[The New York Times](30680.md)* in 2023

[^cite_note-NYT2023-5]

With the support of Sera and [talent scout](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Scout_\(sport\)) Rob McIntyre, Piastri moved to compete in [Europe](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_kart_racing_championships#Europe) from 2015 onwards with [Kosmic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kosmic_Kart).[^cite_note-22][^cite_note-AS2024-8] *Vroomkart* later contrasted his career path to that of [Ayrton Senna](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ayrton_Senna), having both progressed from RC racing to European karting after a transcontinental move.[^cite_note-23] In [KF-Junior](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/KF-Junior), he claimed twelfth in the [European Championship](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Karting_European_Championship) round at [Portimão](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Algarve_International_Circuit)—where he finished twenty-sixth overall—and entered one round of the [WSK Super Master Series](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/WSK_Super_Master_Series).[^cite_note-FS2017-24][^cite_note-KAU2015-21] In 2016, he returned to Australia to claim his third successive City of Melbourne Titles.[^cite_note-BRDC-20] In Europe, he joined *Ricky Flynn Motorsport* in [OK-Junior](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/OK-Junior),[^cite_note-KAU2015-21] where he finished tenth in the South Garda Winter Cup, twelfth in the WSK Super Master Series, and eighth in the [WSK Final Cup](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/WSK_Final_Cup).[^cite_note-FS2017-24][^cite_note-25] Back-to-back [podiums](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Podium#In_motorsport) at Portimão saw him claim sixteenth in the European Championship.[^cite_note-26][^cite_note-27] He qualified fourth for the [World Championship](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Karting_World_Championship) at [Sakhir](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bahrain_International_Circuit) after winning his [heat](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Glossary_of_motorsport_terms#H); his pre-final saw him drop to eighteenth before recovering to finish sixth overall in his final karting appearance.[^cite_note-28][^cite_note-29]&#x20;

### Formula 4 (2016–2017)

#### 2016–2017: Junior formulae debut in the UAE

Following his sixth-place at the [Karting World Championship](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Karting_World_Championship) in [OK-Junior](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/OK-Junior), Piastri made his [junior formulae](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Junior_formulae) debut in the second round of the [Formula 4 UAE Championship](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2016%E2%80%9317_Formula_4_UAE_Championship) at [Yas Marina](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Yas_Marina_Circuit) with Dragon F4;[^cite_note-30] in a nine-driver field, he claimed sixth, fifth, fourth, and fifth on debut.[^cite_note-FS2022-31][^cite_note-F4UAE17-32] He then scored a pair of fourth-places at [Dubai](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dubai_Autodrome) before claiming two podiums on his return to Yas Marina—his final appearance in the series, ending the season sixth overall having contested 11 of 18 races.[^cite_note-F4UAE17-32][^cite_note-33][^cite_note-SC2023-13]&#x20;

#### 2017: Runner-up in British F4

Piastri driving the Mygale M14-F4 during the 2017 F4 British Championship

Piastri finished runner-up to [Jamie Caroline](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jamie_Caroline) in his rookie [British F4](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2017_F4_British_Championship) season.

Piastri moved to the [F4 British Championship](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/F4_British_Championship) for [2017](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2017_F4_British_Championship) with [Arden](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Arden_International),[^cite_note-34] owned by [Christian Horner](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Christian_Horner)—who later revealed he turned down the chance to sign Piastri to the [Red Bull Junior Team](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Red_Bull_Junior_Team).[^cite_note-35] He opened his campaign with two podiums at [Brands Hatch](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Brands_Hatch),[^cite_note-36] which he repeated in the third race at [Donington Park](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Donington_Park), marred by the accident of [Billy Monger](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Billy_Monger).[^cite_note-37][^cite_note-38] After another podium at [Thruxton](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Thruxton_Circuit),[^cite_note-39] he claimed his maiden F4 victory in the second race at [Oulton Park](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Oulton_Park) to move second in the championship.[^cite_note-40][^cite_note-41] Podiums in each race at [Croft](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Croft_Circuit) and two victories from [pole position](630713.md) each at [Snetterton](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Snetterton_Circuit) and [Knockhill](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Knockhill) consolidated his position.[^cite_note-42][^cite_note-43] He came under pressure from [Logan Sargeant](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Logan_Sargeant) after two retirements at [Rockingham](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rockingham_Motor_Speedway).[^cite_note-44] A triple podium at [Silverstone](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Silverstone_Circuit), including victory in the final race, extended his advantage,[^cite_note-AA-SIL-45][^cite_note-46] before he clinched the runner-up spot to the second-year [Jamie Caroline](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jamie_Caroline) with top-five finishes at Brands Hatch.[^cite_note-47] He closed the season on 376.5 points—20.5 ahead of Sargeant and 65.5 behind Caroline—with six victories from 15 podiums and six pole positions.[^cite_note-48] He later credited "learning from \[his] mistakes in F4" as his greatest lesson in [junior formulae](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Junior_formulae).[^cite_note-49]&#x20;

### Formula Renault 2.0 (2017–2019)

#### 2017: Northern European Cup debut

Following his [British F4](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2017_F4_British_Championship) campaign, Piastri debuted in the [Formula Renault Northern European Cup](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2017_Formula_Renault_Northern_European_Cup) at the season-ending [Hockenheimring](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hockenheimring) round with [Arden](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Arden_International).[^cite_note-50] He claimed eighth in both races, ending the season twenty-first overall.[^cite_note-FS2022-31][^cite_note-51][^cite_note-FRNEC17-52] He was subsequently invited to attend the [Formula Renault 2.0](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Formula_Renault_2.0) rookie test at [Barcelona-Catalunya](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Circuit_de_Barcelona-Catalunya), setting the fifth-fastest time.[^cite_note-53]&#x20;

#### 2018: Podiums in the Eurocup

Piastri driving the Tatuus FR2.0/13 at the 2018 Formula Renault Eurocup Monza round

In [Formula Renault 2.0](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Formula_Renault_2.0), Piastri took three podiums across his [Eurocup](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Formula_Renault_Eurocup) campaign in [2018](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2018_Formula_Renault_Eurocup).

Piastri remained with [Arden](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Arden_International) for his [Eurocup](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Formula_Renault_Eurocup) debut in [2018](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2018_Formula_Renault_Eurocup).[^cite_note-54] After claiming sixth and fifth on debut at [Paul Ricard](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Circuit_Paul_Ricard),[^cite_note-55][^cite_note-56] he scored no points at [Monza](952083.md), including a retirement.[^cite_note-AA-MNZ-57] He finished fourth in the second race at [Silverstone](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Silverstone_Circuit) before dropping outside of the points again in [Monaco](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Circuit_de_Monaco).[^cite_note-58][^cite_note-59] Following his sixth- and ninth-placed finishes at the [Red Bull Ring](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Red_Bull_Ring),[^cite_note-60] Piastri achieved his maiden podium finish at [Spa-Francorchamps](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Spa-Francorchamps) with third in the opening race.[^cite_note-AA-SPA-61] Back-to-back points at the [Hungaroring](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hungaroring) extended his run of points finishes to six races,[^cite_note-AA-HUN-62] which ended in the first race at the [Nürburgring](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/N%C3%BCrburgring); he claimed seventh in race two.[^cite_note-63] With podiums in both races at the [Hockenheimring](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hockenheimring)—finishing third and second—he surpassed [Alex Peroni](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Alex_Peroni) for eighth in the championship,[^cite_note-64] which he retained amidst non-scoring races at the season-ending [Barcelona-Catalunya](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Circuit_de_Barcelona-Catalunya) round.[^cite_note-FS2018-65] He closed his campaign with 110 points—165.5 behind champion [Max Fewtrell](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Max_Fewtrell)—and three podiums.[^cite_note-FS2018-65][^cite_note-66]&#x20;

#### 2019: Maiden title

Piastri with his trophy and champagne after winning the 2019 Formula Renault Eurocup Spa-Francorchamps round

Piastri won the Eurocup in [2019](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2019_Formula_Renault_Eurocup) amidst a title battle with [Victor Martins](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Victor_Martins), his first junior championship.

Piastri—now competing [under a British licence](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Motorsport_UK)—joined reigning champions [R-ace GP](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/R-ace_GP) for the [2019 Eurocup](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2019_Formula_Renault_Eurocup).[^cite_note-67][^cite_note-68] His campaign opened with eighteenth and fourth at [Monza](952083.md).[^cite_note-AA-MNZ-57] He took his maiden victory from [pole position](630713.md) in the first race at [Silverstone](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Silverstone_Circuit), which he repeated in the second.[^cite_note-69] He claimed fourth and fifth in [Monaco](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Circuit_de_Monaco),[^cite_note-70] before returning to the podium with second at [Paul Ricard](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Circuit_Paul_Ricard), where he finished sixth in race two.[^cite_note-71] He closed [Victor Martins](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Victor_Martins)'s championship lead to two points with victory in the opening race at [Spa-Francorchamps](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Spa-Francorchamps) and levelled the standings with his fourth-place in race two.[^cite_note-72][^cite_note-73] He opened a 40-point advantage by winning both races at the [Nürburgring](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/N%C3%BCrburgring) from pole.[^cite_note-74][^cite_note-75] A [formation lap](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Formation_lap) crash at the [Hungaroring](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hungaroring) after [aquaplaning](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Aquaplaning) reduced the gap by 12.5 points with Martins's victory,[^cite_note-77] which he mitigated by winning the second race from pole.[^cite_note-AA-HUN-62] Martins closed his lead to 5.5 points with three consecutive wins from pole at the penultimate [Barcelona-Catalunya](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Circuit_de_Barcelona-Catalunya) and [Hockenheimring](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hockenheimring) rounds as Piastri finished fifth, third, and second, the latter after an early battle for the lead—his second-place in race two extended it by eight.[^cite_note-FS2019-78][^cite_note-79] At [Yas Marina](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Yas_Marina_Circuit), Piastri held off the late advances of Martins to win the opening race, meaning he would clinch the title by finishing seventh or higher in the final race, irrespective of other results.[^cite_note-80] With fourth-place, Piastri clinched his first [junior formulae](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Junior_formulae) championship by 7.5 points to Martins, having achieved seven wins from 11 podiums and five pole positions.[^cite_note-81][^cite_note-FS2019-78] Bruce Williams of *[Auto Action](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Auto_Action)* described it as a "a considerable step towards a Formula One career".[^cite_note-82]&#x20;

### FIA Formula 3 (2020)

In [2018](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2018_GP3_Series), Piastri partook in the [GP3 Series](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/GP3_Series) post-season test at [Yas Marina](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Yas_Marina_Circuit) with [Trident](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Trident_Motorsport).[^cite_note-84][^cite_note-85] He returned for the [2019](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2019_FIA_Formula_3_Championship) post-season test with reigning [FIA Formula 3](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/FIA_Formula_3) champions [Prema](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Prema_Racing).[^cite_note-86] Now [managed](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sports_agent) by [Mark Webber](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mark_Webber_\(racing_driver\)) and a member of the [Renault Sport Academy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Renault_Sport_Academy),[^cite_note-AS2025-1-12][^cite_note-MS2020-87] he signed with Prema for [2020](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2020_FIA_Formula_3_Championship) alongside former [F4](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Formula_4) rival [Logan Sargeant](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Logan_Sargeant) and reigning [Formula Regional European](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2019_Formula_Regional_European_Championship) champion [Frederik Vesti](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Frederik_Vesti).[^cite_note-88] The season was [delayed and shortened](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Impact_of_the_COVID-19_pandemic_on_motorsport#Formula_2_and_Formula_3) due to the [COVID-19 pandemic](62750956.md).[^cite_note-89] Stating that "fighting for the championship definitely is not out of the question",[^cite_note-90] Piastri qualified third on debut in [Spielberg](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2020_Spielberg_Formula_3_round); avoiding damage in a first-corner collision with polesitter [Sebastián Fernández](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sebasti%C3%A1n_Fern%C3%A1ndez_\(racing_driver\)), he passed [Lirim Zendeli](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lirim_Zendeli) at *[Ams Ag](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Red_Bull_Ring)* to claim a debut [feature race](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/FIA_Formula_3_Championship#Race_weekend) victory,[^cite_note-91] before finishing eighth in the [reverse-grid](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Glossary_of_motorsport_terms#R) sprint.[^cite_note-93][^cite_note-94] At the [second Spielberg round](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2020_2nd_Spielberg_Formula_3_round), he claimed fourth in the curtailed feature and fifth in the sprint to retain his championship lead over Vesti and Sargeant.[^cite_note-95][^cite_note-96] Back-to-back second-placed finishes in [Budapest](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2020_Budapest_Formula_3_round)—as well as in the [Silverstone](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2020_Silverstone_Formula_3_round) feature—increased his advantage to 25 points, prior to losing eight with a [drag reduction system](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Drag_reduction_system) failure in the latter sprint.[^cite_note-AA-HUN-62][^cite_note-AA-SIL-45][^cite_note-97] He fell a point behind Sargeant after claiming seventh and sixth at the [second Silverstone round](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2020_2nd_Silverstone_Formula_3_round) following reliability issues in qualifying,[^cite_note-98][^cite_note-99] which he retained in [Barcelona](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2020_Barcelona_Formula_3_round) by recovering from a sixth-placed feature to win the sprint, courtesy of four opening-lap overtakes.[^cite_note-100][^cite_note-101]&#x20;

At [Spa-Francorchamps](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2020_Spa-Francorchamps_Formula_3_round), Sargeant extended his lead to seven points over Piastri—who claimed fifth and sixth, the latter after a penalty for an illegal overtake on [Richard Verschoor](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Richard_Verschoor)—with his sprint victory.[^cite_note-AA-SPA-61][^cite_note-102] Piastri reclaimed the lead at the penultimate [Monza](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2020_Monza_Formula_3_round) round, where he climbed from fifteenth to third in the feature; he retired from the sprint after a collision with [Clément Novalak](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cl%C3%A9ment_Novalak) and [David Schumacher](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/David_Schumacher_\(racing_driver\)) at *[Variante del Rettifilo](952083.md)*, which was neutralised by Sargeant's late crash with Vesti.[^cite_note-AA-MNZ-57][^cite_note-103] Eight points above Sargeant and 24 above the [ART](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/ART_Grand_Prix) of [Théo Pourchaire](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Th%C3%A9o_Pourchaire) into the season-ending [Mugello](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2020_Mugello_Formula_3_round) round,[^cite_note-104] Piastri—carrying over a five-place [grid penalty](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Glossary_of_motorsport_terms#G) for forcing [David Beckmann](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/David_Beckmann) off-track—lined up sixteenth on the feature [grid](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Glossary_of_motorsport_terms#G);[^cite_note-105] unable to score points in eleventh, he entered the sprint tied with Sargeant—who started fifth—and nine points clear of Pourchaire in eighth.[^cite_note-106] Sargeant was eliminated in a first-lap collision with Zendeli at *[Luco](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mugello_Circuit)* as Piastri passed four drivers, including Pourchaire, for seventh.[^cite_note-107] Pourchaire reclaimed the position on the [safety car](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Safety_car) restart, but was unable to close the gap with his podium as Piastri clinched seventh and the title.[^cite_note-108] He closed his campaign on 164 points—three ahead of Pourchaire and four ahead of Sargeant—with two victories from six podiums.[^cite_note-109] He credited improvements in his racecraft with helping him deal with the increased competition and subsequently became a Formula One [test driver](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Test_driver) for [Renault](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Renault_in_Formula_One).[^cite_note-110][^cite_note-AA2020-111]&#x20;

### FIA Formula 2 (2021)

Piastri driving the Dallara F2 2018 at the 2021 Silverstone Formula 2 round

In [2021](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2021_Formula_2_Championship), Piastri won the [FIA Formula 2 Championship](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/FIA_Formula_2_Championship) in his rookie season with [Prema](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Prema_Racing).

Piastri remained with [Prema](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Prema_Racing) for his [2021](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2021_Formula_2_Championship) campaign in [FIA Formula 2](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/FIA_Formula_2), replacing [Mick Schumacher](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mick_Schumacher) to partner [Ferrari Driver Academy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ferrari_Driver_Academy) member [Robert Shwartzman](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Robert_Shwartzman).[^cite_note-112] He debuted in [Formula Two](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Formula_Two) machinery during the three-day [2020](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2020_Formula_2_Championship) post-season test at [Sakhir](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bahrain_International_Circuit).[^cite_note-113] Prior to the season, he stated that he was expecting a "very challenging year", with plans to remain in the category for two years.[^cite_note-114] Piastri qualified seventh on debut at [Sakhir](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2021_Sakhir_Formula_2_round),[^cite_note-115][^cite_note-116] finishing fifth in the [reverse-grid](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Glossary_of_motorsport_terms#R) first [sprint race](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/FIA_Formula_2_Championship#Race_weekend).[^cite_note-117] Starting sixth for the second sprint,[^cite_note-119] he took his maiden victory after seven overtakes in the closing seven laps, following a [pit stop](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pit_stop) for [option tyres](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Glossary_of_motorsport_terms#O) behind the [safety car](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Safety_car), claiming the lead from [Zhou Guanyu](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Zhou_Guanyu) on the final tour.[^cite_note-120] He then made several overtakes to lead the [feature](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/FIA_Formula_2_Championship#Race_weekend), before retiring amidst a collision with [Dan Ticktum](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dan_Ticktum) while battling for third on the third from last lap.[^cite_note-121] Qualifying third in both [Monte Carlo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2021_Monte_Carlo_Formula_2_round) and [Baku](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2021_Baku_Formula_2_round),[^cite_note-124] he claimed second in the second sprint and feature at the former, as well as the latter feature.[^cite_note-125][^cite_note-126][^cite_note-127] Following his maiden [pole position](630713.md) at [Silverstone](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2021_Silverstone_Formula_2_round), he finished sixth, fourth, and third to claim the championship lead from Zhou.[^cite_note-128][^cite_note-129]&#x20;

After ending fourth and seventh in the [Monza](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2021_Monza_Formula_2_round) sprints, Piastri won the feature from pole in a battle with Zhou, which he repeated at [Sochi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2021_Sochi_Formula_2_round) with [Théo Pourchaire](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Th%C3%A9o_Pourchaire).[^cite_note-AA-MNZ-57][^cite_note-130] He took two further victories at [Jeddah](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2021_Jeddah_Formula_2_round): he won the second sprint and the feature from pole, the latter being aborted in the wake of several [red flag](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Red_flag_\(motorsport\)) incidents.[^cite_note-131] His fifth consecutive pole at [Yas Island](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2021_Yas_Island_Formula_2_round) saw him start tenth in the first sprint, where he claimed third in a late battle with teammate and runner-up Shwartzman to clinch the title;[^cite_note-132][^cite_note-133] he closed the season with his record fourth successive feature victory.[^cite_note-134] Across his campaign, Piastri took 252.5 points—60.5 ahead of Shwartzman—with six wins from 11 podiums and five pole positions.[^cite_note-135][^cite_note-136] He became the sixth driver in history to win the [GP2](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/GP2_Series)/F2 title in their rookie season,[^cite_note-138] the third to win the [GP3](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/GP3_Series)/[F3](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/FIA_Formula_3_Championship) and GP2/F2 titles in successive seasons,[^cite_note-140] and the first to win [Formula Renault](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Formula_Renault), [Formula Three](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Formula_Three), and [Formula Two](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Formula_Two)—or equivalent—championships in successive seasons.[^cite_note-SC2023-13] Piastri was named [FIA Rookie of the Year](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/FIA_Prize_Giving_Ceremony#Rookie_of_the_Year) for his efforts in 2021,[^cite_note-FIAROTY-141] stating "\[he had] done basically everything \[he] could \[to prove himself]".[^cite_note-142]&#x20;

## Formula One career

### Renault / Alpine test driver (2020–2022)

In January 2020, three months after clinching the [Formula Renault Eurocup](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2019_Formula_Renault_Eurocup), Piastri became a member of the [Renault Sport Academy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Renault_Sport_Academy).[^cite_note-MS2020-87] He conducted his first test with [Renault](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Renault_in_Formula_One) upon winning the [FIA Formula 3 Championship](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2020_FIA_Formula_3_Championship) that year, completing nearly 500 km (310 mi) in the [R.S.18](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Renault_R.S.18) at [Sakhir](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bahrain_International_Circuit).[^cite_note-AA2020-111] He retained his place at the re-branded [Alpine Academy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Alpine_Academy) in 2021, for whom he tested the [A521](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Alpine_A521) at the [Yas Marina](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Yas_Marina_Circuit) post-season test,[^cite_note-143] as well as the R.S.18 again at [Silverstone](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Silverstone_Circuit) and [Monza](952083.md).[^cite_note-144][^cite_note-AA-MNZ-57]&#x20;

After his [FIA Formula 2](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2021_Formula_2_Championship) title victory, Piastri was appointed the reserve driver of [Alpine](65240620.md) for their [2022](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2022_Formula_One_World_Championship) campaign, amidst links to an [Alfa Romeo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Alfa_Romeo_in_Formula_One) move;[^cite_note-145] he was also made available in the role for [McLaren](20994.md) following an agreement between the two teams.[^cite_note-146] He completed around 3,000 km (1,900 mi) of further private tests in the A521 throughout the season—part of an "intense" training programme.[^cite_note-147][^cite_note-148] Prior to the [São Paulo Grand Prix](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2022_S%C3%A3o_Paulo_Grand_Prix), he completed a private two-day test for McLaren at [Paul Ricard](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Circuit_Paul_Ricard) in the [MCL35M](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/McLaren_MCL35M), before entering post-season testing in the [MCL36](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/McLaren_MCL36);[^cite_note-149] he conducted his final rookie test with the team across two days at [Barcelona-Catalunya](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Circuit_de_Barcelona-Catalunya).[^cite_note-150]&#x20;

#### 2022 contract dispute

Fernando Alonso driving the Alpine A522 at the 2022 Austrian Grand Prix, viewed in a window's reflection

[Alpine](65240620.md) falsely claimed Piastri had signed for them in 2022, leading to a widely publicised contract dispute.

In June 2022, Piastri was offered a [seat](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Glossary_of_motorsport_terms#S) with [Williams](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Williams_Racing) for [2023](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2023_Formula_One_World_Championship) on a two-year [loan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Loan_\(sports\)) from Alpine, who were expected to retain [Esteban Ocon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Esteban_Ocon) and [Fernando Alonso](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fernando_Alonso).[^cite_note-151] In August, Alonso announced that he would replace the retiring [Sebastian Vettel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sebastian_Vettel) at [Aston Martin](63473867.md).[^cite_note-152] Alpine then announced that Piastri would be his replacement, in a [press release](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Press_release) absent of comments from Piastri himself.[^cite_note-153] He rejected their announcement two hours later via [Twitter](9988187.md), stating that he had not signed a contract and would not be driving for them in 2023.[^cite_note-PR2022-154] Team principal [Otmar Szafnauer](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Otmar_Szafnauer) criticised his actions and "integrity as a human being", stating that he expected loyalty from Piastri and further threatened to take legal action.[^cite_note-155][^cite_note-156] It soon emerged that Piastri was instead in talks with [McLaren](20994.md).[^cite_note-157]&#x20;

> I understand that, without my agreement, [Alpine F1](65240620.md) have put out a press release late this afternoon that I am driving for them next year. This is wrong and I have not signed a contract with Alpine for [2023](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2023_Formula_One_World_Championship). I will not be driving for Alpine next year.&#x20;

— Piastri via [Twitter](9988187.md), denying the Alpine press release two hours later

[^cite_note-PR2022-154]

A hearing of the [FIA Contract Recognition Board](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/FIA_Contract_Recognition_Board) (CRB) commenced four weeks later to determine whether Piastri was contracted to Alpine, or if he was a [free agent](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Free_agent)—victory for Alpine could have obligated Piastri to either fulfil the contract for 2023, or required an interested team to activate a [release clause](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Release_clause) for his services.[^cite_note-158] Prior to the [Dutch Grand Prix](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2022_Dutch_Grand_Prix), the CRB ruled against Alpine, followed by an immediate announcement that Piastri would instead join McLaren.[^cite_note-2023contract-159] In their final judgment, the CRB revealed that Piastri had signed his McLaren contract almost a month prior to the Alpine announcement—4 July, the day after the [British Grand Prix](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2022_British_Grand_Prix).[^cite_note-160] He was initially only guaranteed a reserve role, prior to the termination of [Daniel Ricciardo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Daniel_Ricciardo) in the week before the CRB hearing.[^cite_note-161] Piastri later claimed a "breakdown in trust" between him and Alpine was behind his decision to leave.[^cite_note-162]&#x20;

### McLaren (2023–present)

#### 2023: Rookie season and maiden podium

Piastri driving the McLaren MCL60 at the 2023 Austrian Grand Prix

Piastri (pictured at the [Austrian Grand Prix](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2023_Austrian_Grand_Prix)) debuted in Formula One with [McLaren](20994.md) in [2023](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2023_Formula_One_World_Championship).

Piastri signed for [McLaren](20994.md) in [2023](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2023_Formula_One_World_Championship), replacing fellow Australian [Daniel Ricciardo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Daniel_Ricciardo) to partner [Lando Norris](48978813.md).[^cite_note-2023contract-159] On debut at the [Bahrain Grand Prix](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2023_Bahrain_Grand_Prix), Piastri qualified eighteenth amidst performance concerns with the [MCL60](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/McLaren_MCL60), before retiring from twelfth with an electrical issue.[^cite_note-163] He started eighth in [Saudi Arabia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2023_Saudi_Arabian_Grand_Prix) prior to dropping outside the [points](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_Formula_One_World_Championship_points_scoring_systems) with [front wing](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Formula_One_car#Wings) damage sustained in a first-lap collision with [Pierre Gasly](40843850.md).[^cite_note-164] His maiden points finish came at [his home Grand Prix in Australia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2023_Australian_Grand_Prix), claiming eighth after starting sixteenth.[^cite_note-165] He finished outside the points with eleventh and nineteenth at the [Azerbaijan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2023_Azerbaijan_Grand_Prix) and [Miami](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2023_Miami_Grand_Prix) Grands Prix, respectively, suffering contact with [Alexander Albon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Alexander_Albon) at the former and reliability issues at the latter.[^cite_note-166][^cite_note-167] A tenth-placed finish saw him claim a point in [Monaco](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2023_Monaco_Grand_Prix),[^cite_note-168] before a run of three further non–point finishes—in [Spain](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2023_Spanish_Grand_Prix), [Canada](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2023_Canadian_Grand_Prix), and [Austria](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2023_Austrian_Grand_Prix).[^cite_note-169][^cite_note-170][^cite_note-171] Major upgrades for the [British Grand Prix](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2023_British_Grand_Prix) saw Piastri qualify third and finish fourth,[^cite_note-172] before finishing fifth in [Hungary](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2023_Hungarian_Grand_Prix).[^cite_note-173] He qualified second for the [Belgium](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2023_Belgian_Grand_Prix) sprint, retaining the position after a battle for the lead with [Max Verstappen](41758713.md);[^cite_note-174] he retired from the Grand Prix subsequent to a first-corner collision with [Carlos Sainz Jr.](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Carlos_Sainz_Jr.)[^cite_note-175] Following a ninth-place at the [Dutch Grand Prix](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2023_Dutch_Grand_Prix),[^cite_note-176] he finished twelfth in [Italy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2023_Italian_Grand_Prix) upon [front wing](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Downforce) damage from [Lewis Hamilton](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lewis_Hamilton), taking his maiden [fastest lap](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_Formula_One_drivers_who_set_a_fastest_lap) after the resultant [pit stop](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pit_stop).[^cite_note-177][^cite_note-178] He qualified seventeenth in [Singapore](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2023_Singapore_Grand_Prix) after a [red flag](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Red_flag_\(motorsport\)), where he recovered to seventh.[^cite_note-179] Finishing third after starting on the [front-row](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Glossary_of_motorsport_terms#F) at the [Japanese Grand Prix](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2023_Japanese_Grand_Prix), he became the first rookie to achieve a podium finish since [Lance Stroll](42274711.md) in [2017](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2017_Azerbaijan_Grand_Prix).[^cite_note-180] He then took pole for the [Qatar Grand Prix](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2023_Qatar_Grand_Prix) sprint, holding off Verstappen to claim his maiden [sprint victory](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_Formula_One_sprint_winners);[^cite_note-181] he finished second in the main race, having started sixth.[^cite_note-182] He retired from the [United States Grand Prix](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2023_United_States_Grand_Prix) following a collision with [Esteban Ocon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Esteban_Ocon),[^cite_note-183] and claimed eighth in [Mexico City](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2023_Mexico_City_Grand_Prix).[^cite_note-184] He sustained first-lap damage enroute to fourteenth in [São Paulo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2023_S%C3%A3o_Paulo_Grand_Prix),[^cite_note-185] before closing his rookie season with tenth- and sixth-placed finishes in [Las Vegas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2023_Las_Vegas_Grand_Prix) and [Abu Dhabi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2023_Abu_Dhabi_Grand_Prix)—qualifying third at the latter.[^cite_note-186][^cite_note-187] Piastri finished ninth in the [World Drivers' Championship](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/World_Drivers%27_Championship) with two podiums and 97 points, 108 behind teammate Norris in sixth;[^cite_note-SS2023-188] his results led to a multi-year contract extension with McLaren until at least the end of 2026.[^cite_note-189]&#x20;

#### 2024: Maiden wins

Piastri driving the McLaren MCL38 at the 2024 Chinese Grand Prix

Piastri (pictured at the [Chinese Grand Prix](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2024_Chinese_Grand_Prix)) retained his [seat](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Glossary_of_motorsport_terms#S) in [2024](72504121.md) as McLaren emerged as challengers to [Red Bull](1173193.md).

Piastri opened his [2024](72504121.md) campaign with eighth at the [Bahrain Grand Prix](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2024_Bahrain_Grand_Prix),[^cite_note-190] followed by fourth-placed finishes in [Saudi Arabia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2024_Saudi_Arabian_Grand_Prix) and [Australia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2024_Australian_Grand_Prix).[^cite_note-191][^cite_note-192] He claimed eighth again at both the [Japanese](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2024_Japanese_Grand_Prix) and [Chinese](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2024_Chinese_Grand_Prix) Grands Prix.[^cite_note-193][^cite_note-194] In [Miami](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2024_Miami_Grand_Prix), McLaren emerged as challengers to championship leaders [Red Bull](1173193.md);[^cite_note-195] Piastri dropped to thirteenth following a collision in his battle for second with [Carlos Sainz Jr.](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Carlos_Sainz_Jr.), as teammate [Lando Norris](48978813.md) took victory.[^cite_note-196] He qualified second at the [Emilia Romagna Grand Prix](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2024_Emilia_Romagna_Grand_Prix)—demoted to fifth due to a [grid penalty](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Glossary_of_motorsport_terms#G) for impeding [Kevin Magnussen](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kevin_Magnussen)—and finished fourth.[^cite_note-197] Piastri qualified and finished second to [Charles Leclerc](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Charles_Leclerc) at the [Monaco Grand Prix](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2024_Monaco_Grand_Prix),[^cite_note-198] prior to claiming fifth and seventh at the [Canadian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2024_Canadian_Grand_Prix) and [Spanish](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2024_Spanish_Grand_Prix) Grands Prix, respectively.[^cite_note-199][^cite_note-200] He finished second in both the sprint and main race at the [Austrian Grand Prix](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2024_Austrian_Grand_Prix).[^cite_note-201] In [Britain](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2024_British_Grand_Prix), he climbed to second from fifth in the wet before a belated switch to [intermediate tyres](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Intermediate_tyres) dropped him to fourth;[^cite_note-202] he fractured a rib during the Grand Prix due to an improper seat fit, which he sustained until the summer break.[^cite_note-203] Piastri qualified second for the [Hungarian Grand Prix](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2024_Hungarian_Grand_Prix) before overtaking teammate Norris into the first corner; McLaren then controversially allowed Norris to [undercut](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Glossary_of_motorsport_terms#U) him prior to [ordering a position-swap](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Team_orders).[^cite_note-204] He allowed Piastri past with two laps remaining, who claimed his maiden [victory](6732460.md) to become the [fifth Australian to win a Formula One Grand Prix](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Formula_One_drivers_from_Australia).[^cite_note-205] Norris commented that the result was "fair" and "honest", conceding that he lost the race at the start.[^cite_note-206]&#x20;

Piastri leads Charles Leclerc at the 2024 Dutch Grand Prix

Piastri (pictured at the [Dutch Grand Prix](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2024_Dutch_Grand_Prix)) took two victories as McLaren won the [World Constructors' Championship](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/World_Constructors%27_Championship).

Piastri finished second in [Belgium](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2024_Belgian_Grand_Prix) after a disqualification for [George Russell](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/George_Russell_\(racing_driver\)), promoting him to fourth in the standings—ahead of Sainz—before finishing fourth at the [Dutch Grand Prix](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2024_Dutch_Grand_Prix).[^cite_note-207][^cite_note-208] He led the majority of the [Italian Grand Prix](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2024_Italian_Grand_Prix) after a first-lap overtake on Norris, before a strategic error saw him finish second to the [one-stop](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pit_stop) of Leclerc.[^cite_note-209] Qualifying second to Leclerc in [Azerbaijan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2024_Azerbaijan_Grand_Prix), Piastri claimed another victory following a race-long battle for the lead.[^cite_note-210] He then took third in [Singapore](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2024_Singapore_Grand_Prix),[^cite_note-211] before finishing fifth at the [United States Grand Prix](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2024_United_States_Grand_Prix).[^cite_note-212] Eighth-placed finishes at the [Mexico City](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2024_Mexico_City_Grand_Prix) and [São Paulo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2024_S%C3%A3o_Paulo_Grand_Prix) Grands Prix—including second at the latter sprint after being ordered to allow Norris to win—were followed by seventh-place in [Las Vegas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2024_Las_Vegas_Grand_Prix) after a [false start](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/False_start) penalty.[^cite_note-213][^cite_note-214][^cite_note-215][^cite_note-216] Norris returned the favour at the [chequered flag](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chequered_flag) for the [Qatar Grand Prix](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2024_Qatar_Grand_Prix) sprint, allowing Piastri to take victory before he finished third in the main race.[^cite_note-217][^cite_note-218] He finished the season-ending [Abu Dhabi Grand Prix](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2024_Abu_Dhabi_Grand_Prix) in tenth after a first-lap collision with [Max Verstappen](41758713.md).[^cite_note-219] Piastri ended the season fourth in the World Drivers' Championship on 292 [points](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_Formula_One_World_Championship_points_scoring_systems)—82 behind runner-up Norris—with two victories from eight podiums, helping McLaren win their first [World Constructors' Championship](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/World_Constructors%27_Championship) since [1998](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1998_Formula_One_World_Championship).[^cite_note-220]&#x20;

#### 2025: Three-way title battle vs. Norris and Verstappen

Piastri driving the McLaren MCL39 at the 2025 Japanese Grand Prix

Piastri (pictured at the [Japanese Grand Prix](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2025_Japanese_Grand_Prix)) was one of the favourites ahead of the [2025](72768531.md) World Drivers' Championship.

McLaren entered [2025](72768531.md) as title favourites, with Piastri expected to challenge teammate [Lando Norris](48978813.md) for the [World Drivers' Championship](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/World_Drivers%27_Championship).[^cite_note-221] Three days prior to the start of his campaign, McLaren announced a multi-year contract extension with Piastri until at least the end of the 2028 season.[^cite_note-contract-222] He qualified on the [front-row](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Glossary_of_motorsport_terms#F) for the season-opening [Australian Grand Prix](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2025_Australian_Grand_Prix), behind teammate Norris;[^cite_note-223] he challenged Norris for the lead before spinning in wet conditions and leaving the track, ultimately finishing ninth.[^cite_note-224] After finishing second in the sprint, he took his maiden [pole position](24385119.md) at the [Chinese Grand Prix](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2025_Chinese_Grand_Prix), dominating the race ahead of Norris to claim his first victory of the season.[^cite_note-225] He finished third in [Japan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2025_Japanese_Grand_Prix) behind [Max Verstappen](41758713.md) and Norris.[^cite_note-226] Piastri took pole for the [Bahrain Grand Prix](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2025_Bahrain_Grand_Prix), cruising to a 15-second winning margin and moving within three [points](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_Formula_One_World_Championship_points_scoring_systems) of Norris.[^cite_note-227] He took another victory in [Saudi Arabia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2025_Saudi_Arabian_Grand_Prix) to become the [first Australian to lead the World Drivers' Championship](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Formula_One_drivers_from_Australia) since [2010](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2010_Formula_One_World_Championship).[^cite_note-228] He finished second in the [Miami Grand Prix](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2025_Miami_Grand_Prix) sprint after a late [safety car](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Safety_car) put him behind Norris, before winning the main race.[^cite_note-229] On pole for the [Emilia Romagna Grand Prix](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2025_Emilia_Romagna_Grand_Prix), he finished third after a first-corner overtake by Verstappen and a strategic error.[^cite_note-230] He won the [Spanish Grand Prix](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2025_Spanish_Grand_Prix) after starting on pole,[^cite_note-231] and finished fourth in [Canada](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2025_Canadian_Grand_Prix), where championship rival Norris attempted to overtake him and crashed into the [pit wall](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Glossary_of_motorsport_terms#P), extending Piastri's lead to 22 points.[^cite_note-232]&#x20;

Piastri driving the McLaren MCL39 at the 2025 Austrian Grand Prix

A title battle between Piastri and teammate [Lando Norris](48978813.md) emerged at the [Austrian Grand Prix](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2025_Austrian_Grand_Prix).

Piastri finished second in [Austria](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2025_Austrian_Grand_Prix) after a race-long battle with Norris, narrowly avoiding another collision after [locking](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Skid_\(automobile\)) his [brakes](65423.md) at *[Rauch](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Red_Bull_Ring)*.[^cite_note-233] He led the majority of the wet-weather [British Grand Prix](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2025_British_Grand_Prix) before receiving a penalty for inadvertently [brake testing](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Brake_test) Verstappen on a restart, conceding the victory to Norris.[^cite_note-234] He finished second to Verstappen from pole in the [Belgium](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2025_Belgian_Grand_Prix) sprint prior to his victory in the wet–dry Grand Prix, which followed an early overtake on polesitter Norris at the exit of *[Raidillon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Raidillon)*.[^cite_note-235] He qualified second—ahead of Norris—in [Hungary](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2025_Hungarian_Grand_Prix), where Norris beat him to victory on a one-stop strategy.[^cite_note-236] He took pole for the [Dutch Grand Prix](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2025_Dutch_Grand_Prix) and held off Norris until his teammate's late engine failure, achieving his maiden *[grand chelem](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Grand_chelem)* and increasing his advantage to 34 points.[^cite_note-237] Verstappen—104 points behind—re-emerged as a distant title threat in [Italy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2025_Italian_Grand_Prix), where Piastri held third until a slow [pit stop](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pit_stop) for Norris elevated him to second; he was controversially [ordered to cede the position](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Team_orders) and settled for third.[^cite_note-238][^cite_note-239][^cite_note-240] He crashed out of qualifying in [Azerbaijan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2025_Azerbaijan_Grand_Prix); starting ninth, he [jumped the start](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Glossary_of_motorsport_terms#J), [stalled](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Stall_\(engine\)), and crashed into *[Turn Five](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Baku_City_Circuit)* on the opening lap, recording his first retirement [in 45 Grands Prix](13599687.md#Most_consecutive_race_finishes) and conceding six points to Norris, as well as 25 to Verstappen.[^cite_note-241] First-lap contact with Norris in [Singapore](77743466.md) dropped Piastri behind him in fourth, as McLaren clinched the World Constructors' Championship with a [joint-record six remaining Grands Prix](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_Formula_One_constructor_records#Other_constructor_records).[^cite_note-242]&#x20;

Another first-corner collision with Norris in the [United States](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2025_United_States_Grand_Prix) sprint saw both retire after Piastri was hit by [Nico Hülkenberg](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nico_H%C3%BClkenberg) in an attempted [switchback](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Glossary_of_motorsport_terms#S) on Norris for second,[^cite_note-243] which neutralised intra-team "repercussions" Norris was set to face after Singapore;[^cite_note-244] Piastri dropped to fifth in the Grand Prix as Verstappen and Norris—first and second, respectively—closed his advantage to 40 and 14 points.[^cite_note-245] Starting seventh and enduring a mid-race battle with both [Mercedes](25098794.md) drivers in [Mexico City](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2025_Mexico_City_Grand_Prix), he finished fifth again and dropped a point behind the victorious Norris.[^cite_note-246] As Norris dominated in [São Paulo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2025_S%C3%A3o_Paulo_Grand_Prix), Piastri crashed out of third in the sprint in drying conditions at the *[Senna 'S'](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Interlagos_Circuit)* and was penalised for colliding with [Kimi Antonelli](70530918.md)—causing the retirement of [Charles Leclerc](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Charles_Leclerc)—in the Grand Prix, dropping from second to fifth.[^cite_note-247] He was initially classified fourth in [Las Vegas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2025_Las_Vegas_Grand_Prix) before both he and Norris were disqualified for [skid block](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Skid_block) wear, levelling him with race-winner Verstappen.[^cite_note-248] Piastri won the [Qatar](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2025_Qatar_Grand_Prix) sprint event from pole position to gain more points on Norris and Verstappen, and finished second in the main race after a strategic error by McLaren allowed Verstappen to win the Grand Prix ahead of Piastri at Lusail. The results in Qatar meant that Piastri who had led the Drivers' Championship for much of the season had now slipped to third in the standings, two points behind Verstappen. At the [season finale in Abu Dhabi](77743503.md), Piastri qualified third and finished second. As Verstappen won the Grand Prix in Abu Dhabi from pole, with team mate Norris in third, this meant Piastri finished the season third in the standings, 13 points behind eventual champion and McLaren team mate Norris.[^cite_note-249] Piastri finished the season with seven victories and 16 podiums, both records for an Australian driver. He also passed [Daniel Ricciardo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Daniel_Ricciardo) and equalled [Mark Webber](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mark_Webber_\(racing_driver\)) in career victories.[^cite_note-250] Piastri's season has been described by himself as one he can be "proud" of, and one of "mighty rise, but uncontrollable fall" by the media.[^cite_note-251][^cite_note-252]&#x20;

#### 2026: New regulations

Piastri remained with McLaren for [new power unit and chassis regulations](71681684.md#Regulation_changes) in [2026](71681684.md). Having qualified fifth for his home race, the season-opening [Australian Grand Prix](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2026_Australian_Grand_Prix), he crashed on his [reconnaissance lap](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Reconnaissance_lap) due to an unexpected engine surge whilst driving over a kerb and failed to start the race.[^cite_note-spike-253][^cite_note-Pia-254] At the [Chinese Grand Prix](80284337.md), he scored points by finishing sixth in the sprint and then qualified fifth for the race, but again failed to start due to an electrical issue before the [formation lap](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Formation_lap).[^cite_note-255] He finished second at the [Japanese Grand Prix](80284355.md), behind [Kimi Antonelli](70530918.md).[^cite_note-256] At the [Miami Grand Prix](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2026_Miami_Grand_Prix), Piastri placed second in the sprint, before qualifying seventh and finishing third in the main race.[^cite_note-257] He finished fourth in the [Canadian Grand Prix](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2026_Canadian_Grand_Prix) sprint and eleventh in the main race.[^cite_note-258][^cite_note-259] In the first European race of the season, Piastri finished fifth on track at the [Monaco Grand Prix](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2026_Monaco_Grand_Prix), and was promoted to fourth after a time penalty for [Alpine](65240620.md) driver [Pierre Gasly](40843850.md), but lost the position after an appeal that overturned Gasly's penalty.[^cite_note-260] Subsequently, he finished fifth again in [Barcelona-Catalunya](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2026_Barcelona-Catalunya_Grand_Prix),[^cite_note-261] and fourth in [Austria](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2026_Austrian_Grand_Prix).[^cite_note-262] At the [British Grand Prix](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2026_British_Grand_Prix), Piastri placed seventh in the sprint race and qualified eighth for the main race.[^cite_note-263][^cite_note-264] He finished eleventh in the main race, having made contact with [Racing Bulls](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Racing_Bulls) driver [Liam Lawson](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Liam_Lawson) and sustained damage to his car during the opening lap.[^cite_note-265][^cite_note-266] At the [Belgian Grand Prix](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2026_Belgian_Grand_Prix), he finished seventh in qualifying, but started the race sixth due to teammate [Lando Norris'](48978813.md) ten-place grid penalty,[^cite_note-Belgium_2026_Starting_grid-267][^cite_note-268] eventually finishing fifth.[^cite_note-269] Following a three-place grid penalty for [Lewis Hamilton](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lewis_Hamilton), Piastri qualified third for the [Hungarian Grand Prix](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2026_Hungarian_Grand_Prix).[^cite_note-270] He spent multiple laps as the race leader before a collision with [Carlos Sainz Jr.](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Carlos_Sainz_Jr.), and eventually had to retire on the fifty-sixth lap due to a gearbox problem.[^cite_note-271]&#x20;

Following the summer break, Piastri qualified fourth and finished fifth in the sprint for the [Dutch Grand Prix](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2026_Dutch_Grand_Prix).[^cite_note-272][^cite_note-273] He qualified fourth again for the main race, before finishing sixth.[^cite_note-274][^cite_note-275] At the [Italian Grand Prix](81006624.md), he finished third in qualifying, but subsequently incurred a three-place grid penalty for impeding [Liam Lawson](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Liam_Lawson), and finished fifth in the race.[^cite_note-276][^cite_note-277] This was followed by an eighth-place finish at the [Spanish Grand Prix](79774929.md) and a thirteenth-place finish at the [Azerbaijan Grand Prix](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2026_Azerbaijan_Grand_Prix).[^cite_note-278][^cite_note-279]&#x20;

## Driver profile

### Mentality

Piastri driving the McLaren MCL38 at the 2024 Dutch Grand Prix

Piastri (pictured at the [2025 Emilia Romagna Grand Prix](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2025_Emilia_Romagna_Grand_Prix)) has been acclaimed for his composure under pressure.

Piastri has been noted by drivers and critics for his composure under pressure. In [2023](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2023_Formula_One_World_Championship), Scott Mitchell-Malm of *The Race* compared his "relaxed intensity" to that of [Max Verstappen](41758713.md).[^cite_note-TR2023-280] Upon winning the [2024 Azerbaijan Grand Prix](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2024_Azerbaijan_Grand_Prix), Luke Smith of *[The New York Times](30680.md)* stated that "stress isn't an emotion one would associate with Piastri. His calm, collected demeanor has been present right from his junior days."[^cite_note-NYT2024-281] This quality prompted several journalists to list him as a favourite for the [2025](72768531.md) World Drivers' Championship after the [Bahrain Grand Prix](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2025_Bahrain_Grand_Prix),[^cite_note-282][^cite_note-283][^cite_note-AS2025-2-284] with [Martin Brundle](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Martin_Brundle) comparing him to [Alain Prost](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Alain_Prost).[^cite_note-285] Andrew Benson of *[BBC Sport](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/BBC_Sport)* stated "combining consistent speed \[with] mental solidity and racing decisiveness \[makes him] a formidable rival".[^cite_note-286] After winning the [Saudi Arabian Grand Prix](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2025_Saudi_Arabian_Grand_Prix), title rival Verstappen stated "he's very calm in his approach, and I like that. \[...] He delivers when he has to, barely makes mistakes—and that's what you need when you want to fight for a championship".[^cite_note-287] As his title battle with teammate [Lando Norris](48978813.md) emerged, [Natalie Pinkham](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Natalie_Pinkham) of *[Top Gear](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Top_Gear_\(magazine\))* described him as "the cerebral assassin", adding that "he races like he's solving a complex puzzle at \[200 mph (320 km/h)]".[^cite_note-288] His composed demeanour has drawn several comparisons to [Kimi Räikkönen](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kimi_R%C3%A4ikk%C3%B6nen), widely known as *the Iceman*.[^cite_note-289][^cite_note-290][^cite_note-291]&#x20;

### Development

Piastri driving the McLaren MCL39 at the 2025 Japanese Grand Prix

Piastri (pictured at the [2025 Japanese Grand Prix](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2025_Japanese_Grand_Prix)) has been noted for his development across his debut seasons.

Piastri has also been lauded for his adaptability, with race engineer [Tom Stallard](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tom_Stallard) commending his ability to identify flaws in real-time and make improvements without [data analysis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Data_analysis).[^cite_note-TR2023-280] His ability to promptly learn from mistakes was initially noted by his engineer in the [F4 UAE Championship](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2016%E2%80%9317_Formula_4_UAE_Championship).[^cite_note-FS2017-24] *[Sky Sports](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sky_Sports)* described [his rookie campaign](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2023_Formula_One_World_Championship) as "very impressive", noting that his [tyre](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Formula_One_tyres) management required improvement.[^cite_note-SS2023-188] He was noted for his race pace development throughout [2024](72504121.md), as he took his maiden victories in [Hungary](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2024_Hungarian_Grand_Prix) and [Azerbaijan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2024_Azerbaijan_Grand_Prix)—the latter was described by Luke Smith of *[The New York Times](30680.md)* as a "[coming-of-age](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Coming-of-age) drive".[^cite_note-292][^cite_note-293][^cite_note-NYT2024-281] By [2025](72768531.md), several critics opined that he had eradicated the tyre management and qualifying pace issues he experienced in his earlier seasons;[^cite_note-AS2025-2-284][^cite_note-294][^cite_note-295][^cite_note-296] he worked closely with his [engineers](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Race_engineer) to solve the former and extensively analysed his past performances to iterate upon the latter.[^cite_note-297][^cite_note-298]&#x20;

## Personal life

Piastri signing autographs behind a crowd of fans at the 2025 Australian Grand Prix

Piastri with fans at the [2025 Australian Grand Prix](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2025_Australian_Grand_Prix)

Outside motor racing, Piastri is interested in video games—particularly *[Call of Duty](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Call_of_Duty)*—and cooking.[^cite_note-NYT2023-5] He initially adopted the number 11 in [kart racing](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kart_racing) before a rival started using it, prompting his switch to 81 in national competition, [junior formulae](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Junior_formulae), and as his [personal driver number](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_Formula_One_driver_numbers) in Formula One.[^cite_note-299] His [racing helmet](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Racing_helmet) also originates from his karting career, utilising base colours of red and blue with fluorescent yellow details—modified from [off-the-shelf](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ready-to-wear) designs—as well as the [flag of Australia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Flag_of_Australia).[^cite_note-300] He is a supporter of the Melbourne-based [Australian rules football](2403.md) club [Richmond](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Richmond_Football_Club).[^cite_note-301] He also plays [cricket](25675557.md), where he supports the [Australia national team](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Australia_national_cricket_team) and the [Delhi Capitals](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Delhi_Capitals) in the [Indian Premier League](13260340.md)—the latter after a [social media](5897742.md) post asking for recommendations of which team to support;[^cite_note-302] he hosted the national team at the [McLaren Technology Centre](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/McLaren_Technology_Centre) (MTC) following the [2025 ICC World Test Championship final](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2025_World_Test_Championship_final).[^cite_note-303]&#x20;

Since 2018, Piastri has been in a relationship with his [childhood sweetheart](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Childhood_sweetheart), Lily Zneimer, an [engineering](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Engineering) graduate he met while studying at his [sixth form](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sixth_form) in England.[^cite_note-304] During his early career, he lived in an apartment in proximity to the MTC in [Woking](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Woking),[^cite_note-NYT2023-5] before moving to [Monte Carlo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Monte_Carlo) in 2024.[^cite_note-305]&#x20;

[^cite_note-RSA-2]: &#x20;Known until 2021 as the *Renault Sport Academy*.<sup>[\[1\]](#cite_note-1)</sup>

[^cite_note-77]: &#x20;[Victor Martins](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Victor_Martins) was awarded half points for his victory in race one at the [Hungaroring](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hungaroring) as the race was abandoned after three laps due to adverse weather conditions.<sup>[\[75\]](#cite_note-76)</sup>

[^cite_note-84]: &#x20;The [GP3 Series](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/GP3_Series) merged with the [FIA Formula 3 European Championship](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/FIA_Formula_3_European_Championship) to form the [FIA Formula 3 Championship](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/FIA_Formula_3_Championship) in [2019](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2019_FIA_Formula_3_Championship).<sup>[\[81\]](#cite_note-83)</sup>

[^cite_note-93]: &#x20;In the [2020 FIA Formula 3 Championship](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2020_FIA_Formula_3_Championship), the top 10 finishers in the [feature race](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/FIA_Formula_3_Championship#Race_weekend) were subjected to a [reverse-grid](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Glossary_of_motorsport_terms#R) in the [sprint race](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/FIA_Formula_3_Championship#Race_weekend).<sup>[\[89\]](#cite_note-92)</sup>

[^cite_note-119]: &#x20;In the [2021 FIA Formula 2 Championship](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2021_FIA_Formula_2_Championship), the top 10 qualifiers were subjected to a [reverse-grid](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Glossary_of_motorsport_terms#R) in the first [sprint race](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/FIA_Formula_2_Championship#Race_weekend), the results of which were subjected to another reversal for the second sprint.<sup>[\[114\]](#cite_note-118)</sup>

[^cite_note-124]: &#x20;Since [2017](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2017_Monte_Carlo_Formula_2_round), qualifying for the [Monte Carlo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Circuit_de_Monaco) FIA Formula 2 round had been split into two groups; qualifying position in each group decided the starting [grid-row](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Glossary_of_motorsport_terms#G), with the order of each row decided by the fastest time between them.<sup>[\[117\]](#cite_note-122)</sup> Piastri qualified second in his group and, with a faster time than [Dan Ticktum](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dan_Ticktum), was classified third overall.<sup>[\[118\]](#cite_note-123)</sup>

[^cite_note-138]: &#x20;In [2021](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2021_Formula_2_Championship), Piastri became the sixth driver in history to win the [GP2](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/GP2_Series)/[F2](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/FIA_Formula_2_Championship) title in their rookie season, after: [Nico Rosberg](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nico_Rosberg) ([2005](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2005_GP2_Series)), [Lewis Hamilton](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lewis_Hamilton) ([2006](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2006_GP2_Series)), [Nico Hülkenberg](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nico_H%C3%BClkenberg) ([2009](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2009_GP2_Series)), [Charles Leclerc](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Charles_Leclerc) ([2017](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2017_Formula_2_Championship)), and [George Russell](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/George_Russell_\(racing_driver\)) ([2018](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2018_Formula_2_Championship)).<sup>[\[131\]](#cite_note-137)</sup>

[^cite_note-140]: &#x20;In [2021](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2021_Formula_2_Championship), Piastri became the fifth driver in history to win the [GP3](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/GP3_Series)/[F3](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/FIA_Formula_3_Championship) and [GP2](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/GP2_Series)/[F2](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/FIA_Formula_2_Championship) titles in successive seasons, after: [Charles Leclerc](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Charles_Leclerc) and [George Russell](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/George_Russell_\(racing_driver\)).<sup>[\[132\]](#cite_note-139)</sup>
